# Mission: M-wm-wiring

**Status:** HEAD, IDENTIFY, MAP, DERIVE, ARGUE met (Joe's read, 2026-09-25 ~23:55Z: "it looks good to me"); VERIFY entered (the wiring as a whole, by the diagramprover run over the machine's map); INSTANTIATE drafted: the overall wiring first, then the missing parts and hops.
**Owner:** claude-10, accepted 2026-09-25 ~16:50Z ("I've held the flight loop, the `:construction` tick and the read step, and most rows plug into `flight_runner.clj`, which I wrote"); claude-8 leads PROOF-2a and reviews under author ≠ reviewer; Joe to confirm.
**Repo:** futon3c (the mission lives here). Components live in futon2 (`src/futon2/aif`, `scripts/wm`), mathlib4 (`DarkTower/WarMachine`), futon3c (checkers, cascade-real).
**Governing record:** futon2 `holes/labs/wm-contract/PROOF-2a-THEOREM-draft-2026-09-24.md` (66120f4c at drafting): the theorem is completion; its holes are the components below.

## HEAD (Joe, 2026-09-25, emacs-repl; sense preserved, quotes verbatim)

**Operator-voice anchor.** On H-G-target part 2, ~15:30Z: "'constructed candidate' should be on the fly, as you've said, which implies that we need a different way to do the initial selection otherwise we go around constructing cascades for every mission before we can choose one. A cheap way to select missions is needed. This is what the 'outer loop' is supposed to supply. Now, recent thinking on my part is that the outer loop should itself be a cascade, i.e., one global interpretation (if you will) of Cascade Live; but a live one that gives us a way to get unstuck." Then, ~16:10Z: "since that section comes in very modularly with the rest of the material as a kind of HEAD, I think we should set up a new mission M-wm-wiring and start to get all the other components wired."

**What is already felt to be true.** Every PROOF-2a hole that could be closed on its own has been, in its own process, with its own tests and a test-registry warrant (inventory below). Cascade Live regenerates from real data with zero hand-typed rows (`/api/alpha/cascade-real`, spine 229, O1 176, O1×O4 73, held 124, lineage 88 on 2026-09-25). The operator's own turns are being read in pattern terms (M-the-perfect-crime, claude-12: 2,130 of 3,286 records over 2026-08-22..09-21 published). The old outer loop existed and its defects are on record (futon2 `holes/E-outer-loop.md`, aedcc6ae). What is missing is the joining: nothing calls the components in a tick, and the machine has not flown a lifecycle mission under the clauses.

**Anti-glibness discipline.** (1) A hole is closed at component level before it is wired; wiring never closes a hole (Joe, 2026-09-25 ~04:15Z). (2) No new required field or refusal without a red-tape-removal or AIF-validity case. (3) An absence is typed on the record, never a value standing in for it. (4) The outer loop is the machine's, no hand catalog and no imposed ordering (futon2 c2061e27); owner-stated wins over computed. (5) Red tape is a cost term from a measured rate, never a guard. (6) A flight is the test of the wiring and is recorded whether it fails; an accurate record of failure is the deliverable, not a fake success. (7) Each wiring step has its own test and warrant, author ≠ reviewer.

**Working-economy position.** This mission underwrites PROOF-2a's completion: the theorem is proved when the machine flies a mission under Clauses C, D, E, T with the witness conditions checkable on the record. It is underwritten by the components (below), by Cascade Live as the substrate the outer cascade reads, and by the hand-placed first target M-autoclock-in (c2061e27).

**Carried-forward tensions.**
1. The outer cascade's definition is pending OUTER-CASCADE-D (claude-12, invoke-1790349371724, read-only; claude-10 first reader). Until it lands, Clause T's selection has no G for any target; H-G-target part 2 stays open and the mixture law with a seeded record (claude-10, futon2 d615d06e) is its no-data fallback.
2. Which attempt is the grain attempt is unmarked on candidates; the W_c checker requires some successful attempt naming a G_c pass (futon3c 4bc95005). The flight's writer must mark it.
3. The W_c checker's warrant does not cover `grain_gate.clj` and `roles.clj`, loaded by absolute path in the child bb process.
4. Served-by links beyond M-futon-seams are proposed by a reader and verified (futon2 cedf100b): seats propose quotes, the read step turns them into spans; the join of quote to span, and `:quote-ambiguous`, are unbuilt.
5. `sourced-rates`' R5 call site passes nil labels; the click record carries no `:measurement`.
6. Selection reads E from the store the legacy writer and the close rule fill, not from the enactment-habit fold.
7. H-publish's observation exists only at flight time.
8. Counts that produce their rate but are impossible (3/2) reach the kernel (futon2 64f005d4, reach limit).
9. Operator-turn evidence is 17.7% pattern-cited; the join to missions is 4 of 40 by the best-guess script and typed absent by the clock path.
10. RULED (Joe, 2026-09-25 ~17:40Z, futon2 a461b124): a requisition is a different semantic layer from a pending E-/M-/T- job; made at dispatch time, it flags the work in-progress then completed, hence ineligible; eligibility is read from requisition state, not from the target's origin. Done: kimi-task.sh writes the line at mint and on `--complete` (futon3c 25228c0f); all 38 requisition files carry `**Requisition:** completed — …` (futon2 9eddeb42). In flight: the target field reads the line as a typed fact and keeps ineligible entries on the record with their reason (claude-3, TARGET-ELIGIBILITY-I). Clarified (Joe, ~18:05Z): "mooted" not "voted"; creation makes a new T-/E-/M- object eligible, no tag (the `:voted` absence removed, futon2 3ecf7a16). Still open: whether lineage self-caused by the loop's own dispatches (`:dispatched-by`) is excluded from the facts a target is scored on, Joe's to decide.

**Provenance.** Drafted by claude-8 from Joe's emacs-repl turns of 2026-09-25 (~15:30Z, ~16:10Z), the PROOF-2a draft at futon2 66120f4c, and two whistles with claude-12 (~15:55Z, invoke-…a624f627, …f5cd753e).

## Wiring inventory (carried into IDENTIFY)

Closed means: in its own process, tests green, warrant registered. Wiring means: the call from the tick or the flight that does not exist yet. Owner of every wiring row is claude-10 unless noted. Rows 2-9 can be wired against M-autoclock-in before the outer cascade exists: `flight/start` takes the target directly, and the field's entry for M-autoclock-in has next step `:read-criteria`, so its first flight exercises row 2 first (claude-10, 2026-09-25).

| # | hole / variable | component closed at | wiring to do |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | the enactment step | none: `flight.clj` and `flight_runner.clj` have start, read, click, observe and ask, but no step that writes an enactment record | a step that writes the enactment record (attempts, checks, grain, deviations, in the click-001-enactment form); rows 5, 7 and 9 each write into that record, so it comes before them |
| 1 | Target (Clause T) | Lean `TargetGrainG` (mathlib4 759b8ca884, warrants test-registry-eddbe897); OUTER-CASCADE-D landed (futon2 595de935), claude-10's read pending | selection as the outer cascade over the field; the outer cascade supplies the action set (each (target, step kind) with guard and produces, step kinds = the field's own `:next-step` values, plus `defer` and `learn`) and the mixture law from futon2 d615d06e supplies the choice: every target keeps its E mass, targets with a ΔG are re-weighted among themselves, the draw is over EVERY action (never within a cascade-real partition group; the partition is a fact per target on the record, not a stage of selection), and the action set reads only entries with `:eligible true` (futon2 d23f88b9; ineligible entries stay on the record with their reason); the draw's seed and u go on the record with `:law :E-only`, `:g-defined-on #{}`, E's source and the proposer-status table; each executed step records the step kind, the target's facts before and the outcome after, so Q(o | step kind) is measured through `measured-cell` and stays typed absent until it has counts (claude-10's read of OUTER-CASCADE-D, 2026-09-25 ~17:05Z); `:g` per feasible entry citing `deltaG_localises`; pair overlap → comparable or `:incommensurable`; Clause T paragraph |
| 2 | C, read step (H-C, H-C-reach) | extractor v5 (futon2 bf38b5c5, test-registry-e9d8b317); `verify-proposed-link` with `:text-sha256` (cedf100b + 6387fc77 + 1a98d0dc, test-registry-f4576030, non-author test-registry-35e113f3) | click computes outcomes at read time (v5 beside the wants); the text sha is passed, not built. ## IDENTIFY

**Motivation (Joe, 2026-09-25 ~18:20Z, verbatim).** "IDENTIFY as I see it is that 'all the components exist but they are not hooked up'. Now, there's some prior art and discussion here because this is the Nth round of prototyping and previous editions often struggled a lot with exactly this wiring aspect, maybe because the components themselves were not finished. But days and days went by talking about 'refusals' which had to do with contracts (and red tape) that wasn't being specified. So, there's another gap to consider besides hooking things up, which is that it should be effective but also efficient (minimum of red tape)."

**The two gaps.**
1. **Hooking up.** Every component in the inventory is closed in its own process with tests and a warrant, and nothing calls it from a tick or a flight. The machine has not flown a lifecycle mission under the clauses.
2. **Efficiency.** Previous rounds spent their time on refusals whose contracts were never specified. A wiring step that adds a refusal, a required field or a gate adds red tape unless it carries its case. The wiring must be effective (the flight completes or records an accurate failure) and efficient (the fewest contracts a flight must satisfy, each specified before the step that relies on it is built).

**Theoretical anchoring and prior art.**
- The old outer loop turned feasibility from a support condition into a refusal, and when every target was refused the tick abstained (futon2 `holes/E-outer-loop.md` §Defects O3; the loop removed at 5d55e7a0, 2026-09-17). That is the failure mode named above, on the record.
- Rulings that bound the wiring: guards are presumed unwanted and the burden of proof is on the guard (Joe, 2026-09-19); no new required field or refusal without a red-tape-removal or AIF-validity case (Joe, 2026-09-25); an absence is typed on the record, never a value (2026-09-24); the outer loop is the machine's, no hand catalog (futon2 c2061e27); a requisition is a different semantic layer from a pending job, and creation makes a pending object eligible (2026-09-25).
- The PROOF-2 acceptance register (futon2 PROOF-2-THEOREM-draft, AR-1..AR-44) records what each earlier click refused or substituted and why; the reach limits on the component rows of PROOF-2a are typed and each names its case.
- Red tape as a measured quantity: an agent step that went beyond the operator's ask (OUTER-CASCADE-D §1, five cases from the operator-turn blocks); the link field that would measure it is named, not built.
- `holes/labs/wm-contract/NOTE-task-manager-outer-loop.md` (Joe with zai-7, 2026-09-12): the mechanism that pushes a selected item into a lane, and M-autoclock-in as that step.

**Scope.** In: the eleven inventory rows plus row 0, wired against the hand-placed target M-autoclock-in, then the outer cascade's selection of the next target. Out: any new component (a hole found while wiring is closed on its own row in PROOF-2a, never by the wiring); any refusal, required field or gate without its case; the per-mission annotations and figures of the working page until a row is worked.

**Completion criteria (testable).**
1. One flight of M-autoclock-in produces a click record on which every witness condition W₀..Wₜ is checkable and either passes or is typed-refused, with no absence read as a value, and the record is accepted whether the flight succeeded or failed.
2. The outer cascade selects the next target from the eligible field with the draw, its seed and each action's typed G on the record.
3. Every wiring row has its own test and warrant, author ≠ reviewer, and closed no hole.
4. **Efficiency, measured per flight:** (a) the refusals the flight can reach are enumerated in this mission before the flight, each with its case; a refusal reached that is not on the list is a defect of the wiring, not of the target. ENUMERATED (REFUSAL-REGISTER-D, futon2 9681d434, claude-3, read-only at futon2 4c0fedb4): 18 refusals on the flight's path, 12 with a case, 6 without (`:reading/request-not-issued` mission_reading.clj:221; `:nonfinite-g` interpretation_construction.clj:210; `:universe-not-admitted` and `:no-admitted-interpretation` cascade_problems.clj:198-206; `:no-constructed-candidate` :222-231, whose recorded case is for recording the carrier, not for refusing; `:unknown-class` observation_rates.clj:186), each recorded as found for the owner's decision per row; three classes not two: 9 stop the flight, 2 throw and are caught and recorded as a `:need` (flight_runner.clj:127), 7 stop the tick with an abstention on click-summary; beside them 10 kinds of typed absence and 4 flight-ending statuses, one carrying Joe's words in the code that it is not a refusal (flight.clj:277-279). THE WALK on M-autoclock-in (:read-criteria, :criteria-not-stated): no flight-level refusal is on the path a first flight takes (four are guards their callers cannot trigger since settle/revalidate/read-one publish only on :valid; three need a store that lost or contradicts a request the machine wrote; one needs a constraint no caller passes; two are caught); the first thing that would stop it is `:not-a-target-yet` if the readings leave no wants or `:no-progress` after a click that advances none, neither a refusal. Limit: rows 12-18 cover the R1→R14 path of a first click, not the war-machine lane's full tick surface; a refusal reached at an unenumerated tick is not a wiring defect under 4(a) until the register is extended. Two missing WIRES confirmed by the trace (claude-8 verified: no caller outside their own files): `futon2.aif.grain-gate` (row 5) and `futon2.aif.enactment-habit` (row 7) are required by nothing on the flight path. OWNER'S DECISIONS on the six (claude-10, ~19:10Z): five KEEP with a case the register missed: `:reading/request-not-issued` (the same issued-request binding as row 3, PROOF-2a draft :671; a reading publishes without an operator step only because it answers a request the machine issued, else any seat could write criteria unasked; only a lost or contradictory store triggers it); `:universe-not-admitted` (P5 under Joe's 2026-09-17 answer that every token is observed mechanically; dropping unlocated tokens would change the universe G is taken over, the W6 comparability problem; the abstention names `:tokens-without-checkable-locator` and the read step's locator request addresses it); `:no-admitted-interpretation` (P_0/W_0: a candidate's G cannot be recomputed without its patterns' interpretations and receipts; the tick-level counterpart of the field's `:ask-interpretation`); `:no-constructed-candidate` (W_0 requires machine-constructed candidates; selecting a declared hand candidate after the constructor refused would substitute one for the other, which the site's comment rules out); `:unknown-class` (already a `{:status :missing}` return; H-A 7ba427ab: the zero kernel is the unmeasured default only for a class in the contract; a class outside it has no likelihood, and zero would be absence-read-as-value). Four of these (13-16) end the click as a recorded abstention and the flight goes on. One CHANGES: `:nonfinite-g` becomes a typed absence per candidate, `{:g {:absent :nonfinite-g :value v}}`, that candidate left out of the comparison (a NaN in the min comparison picks an arbitrary winner), the constructor's typed result when every candidate is nonfinite; its own packet, author ≠ reviewer: LANDED futon2 f4c1fb09 (claude-13, warrant test-registry-de331a49, interpretation-construction-test 14/100; universe tests 5/32 still green): at HEAD the throw at :210 was already caught into `(refuse :nonfinite-g …)` with `:candidates []`, so one nonfinite G emptied the whole result; now each candidate of the supported family is scored once, a nonfinite one goes under a new `:left-out` slot keeping its precedence, need-edges, target, want, unreached wants and interpretation receipts plus `:g {:absent :nonfinite-g :value v}` (##NaN, ##Inf, :infinite, or the evaluator's refusal map), the comparison runs over the finite ones, `:left-out` is added to a `:constructed` result only when non-empty and gets no construction receipt (that receipt describes a search they were not in); all nonfinite → the file's own refuse shape and kind, `{:status :refused :kind :nonfinite-g :candidates [] :absent :no-finite-g :left-out [...]}`. nf-4 pins the all-finite output byte-identical to a fixture captured from the unchanged source (`test/fixtures/interpretation-construction/nf4-all-finite@futon2-3bbf5059.edn`); a G of 0, 0.0, boxed Double or Long, 0N or 0M is finite; mutation (finite filter removed) 12 failures 2 errors. Owner's review (claude-10, ~19:55Z): accepted, one finding fixed by the owner at futon2 082dfe5e (warrant test-registry-d59fc964, 15/104): `:left-out` was attached only on the `:constructed` branch and vanished on `:construction-not-taken` or a constructor refusal; now on every outcome when non-empty, pinned by nf-5. Nonfinite baseline G: kept as a typed result (no ΔG exists against an undefined baseline, so selecting would substitute a value for a comparison that does not exist; the click abstains, the flight continues), to be made distinguishable from the all-candidates case: follow-up NONFINITE-BASELINE-I for claude-13 (`:absent :nonfinite-baseline-g` with the value; finite candidates carried as data under a key other than `:candidates`, none selected; tests for the distinction and for a finite baseline unchanged). LANDED futon2 ef26bae4 (claude-13, warrant test-registry-21b3bb44, 20/127; universe tests 5/32): the baseline is scored first through the same memoized lookup and checked before the every-candidate-nonfinite case (without a baseline nothing can be compared either way); the result is the refuse shape with kind `:nonfinite-g`, `:absent :nonfinite-baseline-g`, `:baseline-g` (##NaN, ##Inf, :infinite or the evaluator's refusal map), the finite candidates under a new key `:uncompared` in the `:left-out` entry shape each with `:g {:value g :universe u}` (a new key rather than `:left-out` with a reason, so a consumer reading 'these had a nonfinite G' never picks up finite ones and nb-2's two absences are told apart by key alone), `:left-out` for candidates whose own G is nonfinite, `:candidates []`; nb-4 pins a finite baseline byte-identical to a fixture captured from source identical to 082dfe5e; bad case: baseline +Inf and a candidate +Inf yields no ΔG of 0 anywhere (the candidate is left out, nothing constructed, no comparison recorded); mutation (the two absences collapsed) 5 failures. Owner's review (claude-10, ~20:30Z): ACCEPTED as is; the tests pin what was asked; record check by the owner: `:left-out` on this path comes from their 082dfe5e wrapper (nb-1 confirms it adds nothing empty), and `(evaluated c)` is applied only to the finite-filtered family so it cannot throw there. The evaluator's unreachable throw is KEPT with a comment to follow: it is not the kind of guard the ruling targets (it is caught ten lines later into the typed `:nonfinite-g` result the tick records as an abstention; nothing stops), it would become reachable if a move ever created candidates outside `family`, where it prevents a NaN in the min comparison picking an arbitrary winner, and it costs nothing while unreachable. ROW 12 CLOSED. (Correction on the empty-family bad case: it is refused `:invalid-input` before any scoring and does not exercise the baseline path.) Register EXTENDED to the tick (REFUSAL-REGISTER-D §D2, futon2 43476bc8, claude-3, read-only at 3bbf5059; 300 lines total; claude-8 record check: one path on main, the abstention carrier at full_loop_runner.clj:515-521 and `:horizon-not-declared` refusing all targets verified): 25 rows covering 39 kinds over ~60 sites by stage (assembly 6 kinds at cascade_problems.clj:198-234; constructor `:nonfinite-g`, `:construction-not-taken`; scoring: efe.clj's five typed `:status :missing` returns plus observation_rates' two; selection: policy.clj's seven throws, cascade_selection's five kinds; the gate: decision_gate's `:inadmissible-decision` with 14 reasons, controller_authority's `:invalid-controller-authorization` with 4; the judge: war_machine.clj's 19 throw sites). MAIN FINDING: the tick cannot stop the flight; 24 rows stop the tick, one is caught inside the constructor, none stops the flight, since every tick refusal arrives as an abstention on the click record (`abstention-carrier`) that click-summary reads and record-click carries; so 4(a)'s answer is unchanged: only the nine flight-level sites can stop a flight, none on M-autoclock-in's path. 14 rows have a case, 11 none (`:want-not-declared`, `:beta-not-declared`, `:mixed-candidate-kinds`, the F_π input refusals, `:invalid-policy-prefix`, `:precision-consumption-mismatch`, the cascade_selection kinds, the six belief-accumulation kinds, the three precondition gates, the two scoring guards, the two configuration guards), each for the owner per row. The walk continued: after the first criterion publishes, the click abstains at assembly on `:no-admitted-interpretation` (or `:universe-not-admitted` first if no locator reading has landed; cascade_problems takes the first applicable in its documented order); record-click sees no want advanced and the flight ends `:no-progress` unless the ask step published an interpretation in the same click; nothing downstream is reached. Two corrections to D: `:nonfinite-g` was class C not T (caught inside the constructor, surfacing as assembly's `:no-constructed-candidate`), which made the owner's typed-absence decision a smaller change; the efe map opens at :1117. BOUNDARY: `run_ending_classification/classify` catches ExceptionInfo into a typed receipt (:139-143) so the close rule does not throw outward; NOT enumerated are full_loop_runner.clj's 52 further throw sites in the dispatch, build, review and close machinery reached only after a chosen action; a first click never gets there; a flight that does needs a third register before 4(a) covers it. OWNER'S RULINGS (claude-10, ~20:10Z): the eleven no-case tick refusals are "decide when a flight reaches it, recorded then", all of them: D2 shows every tick refusal reaches the click record as an abstention record-click carries, so none stops the flight and none turns an absence into a value, they are already typed absences at the flight's level; a flight reaching one triggers its own row (a case cited, a change to a typed absence, or removal, each its own packet); reviewing eleven cases for code the first flight cannot reach would be the busy work Joe struck. The third register (full_loop_runner's 52 post-choice throw sites): AFTER the first flight, triggered by the first click record that carries a constructed candidate or a chosen action; at that point claude-3's read-only packet goes out and must land BEFORE the next flight, since unlike tick refusals these sites may throw without an abstention carrier and could stop a flight. 4(a) reads: tick: enumerated (§D2); post-choice machinery: ENUMERATED (§D4, futon2 a50e3f52, claude-3, read-only at d6df5909 after step 11 landed under them; 400 lines; claude-8: one path on main, the runner's `catch Throwable` and flight.clj's zero catch sites verified). The runner's 52 post-choice sites have ONE class, checked not assumed: `run-opportunity!` wraps the core in `catch Throwable` (full_loop_runner.clj:5615-5620), types the failure by a stated precedence (explicit `:failure-kind` at any depth, else transport typing, else `:initialization-failed`), records a system-failure finding with a discharge contract and returns a terminal result, so all 52 stop the RUN and are recorded, none stops the flight; three families are re-thrown (`:delivery-qa-gate-failed`, `:evidence-manifest/refusal`, `:limb-evidence/refusal`) and escape the runner; a `:stopping-rule-reached` on the cause chain is `:cohort-complete`, normal completion; listed by family with the outcome census; cases measured not judged: 9 of 52 carry a comment within twelve lines above the throw, 43 'no case found at the site' (a claim about the site only, stated). THE FLIGHT'S ENACTMENT PATH THROWS NOTHING: every branch of `enact-fn` records (a seat's typed failure → `:step-failed` deviation, the next pattern still attempted; a grain-gate refusal stops one commit, the gate's 8 sites carrying 6 kinds; a check-fn exception caught at run-check:470; five typed absences: no decision, no check, no run record, no grain pattern, no publication); `wc-verdict-fn` (flight_runner.clj:583 since d6df5909) gives `{:absent :no-wc-checker-configured}` with `increment` not called, or `{:refused :checker-failed :exit n :stderr s}`, never a verdict; `increment` types a missing verdict as delta 0. WHAT CAN STILL STOP THE FLIGHT: an exception out of an injected function; flight.clj has zero `catch` sites and of the injected fns only `check-fn` is wrapped (`dispatch-step!`, `fetch-run-record`, `publication-observation`, `wc-fn` and the record write are not); on M-autoclock-in's first enactment nothing stops the enactment (the nearest is the gate, stopping one commit) and the first flight-stopper is an exception out of `dispatch-step!`. THE FINDING FOR THE SPIKE: futon2 has NO production `dispatch-step!` (only the two test fixtures; flight_driver.clj mentions it zero times; claude-8 verified by grep), so a seat not on the roster, a job that never returns and the requisition rule surface nowhere in the flight's enactment; the runner's own Agency client carries those refusals (`:agent-unavailable` :757, `:dispatch-failed` :961/:1182, `:cancelled` :1155) but the enactment does not go through the runner; when the spike's dispatch function lands it brings refusals with no register row, a wiring defect under the rule, so they go in the SAME commit as that function. Register total 11 flight-level + 39 tick + 29 post-choice = 79 kinds over ~120 sites; six falsifier greps re-run at d6df5909 all matching (52 / 40 / 3 / 0 / 8 / 0); eight flight_runner line citations corrected after the file moved. ROW 3 wiring fact from the walk: `ask-fn` runs before `click-fn` in `run!`, so the ask step's publication lands inside the click as long as `settle` waits for the seat's answer before the click posts; an answer arriving after the click makes that click read `:no-progress` though the flight is progressing; row 3's test pins both halves (an interpretation published by the ask step is in the click's view; an answer still pending when the click posts is recorded as a pending `:need`, not as no progress); whether today's `agency-answer-fn` blocks until the seat answers is the first thing read when row 3 opens; (b) the count of required fields on the click record does not grow during wiring except by a row that states the case; (c) the number of operator rulings the flight needed before it could complete, target zero after the first; (d) wall time per tick recorded, with the outer cascade's construction cost (OUTER-CASCADE-D §6: ~20 s today) as the baseline it must not silently exceed.

**Relationship to other missions.** Depends on PROOF-2a (the components and their clauses) and on M-autoclock-in (the first target and the clocking step). M-the-perfect-crime supplies the operator-turn evidence the outer cascade reads and the red-tape cases. M-futon-seams is the worked example and a fixture for the read step's link tests, not a target.

## COMPLETE THE MAP, VERIFY THE WIRES (Joe, 2026-09-26 ~11:27Z; no flights until done)

**The ruling.** Components AND wires are verified before any flight runs again. The map is the specification of the remaining work, not a record of it. A flight is a test of the whole and stops at its first defect, so it bounds nothing about what remains (the "each one typed and fixed" correction, 2026-09-26); verification of each wire does.

**JOE (11:35Z): stick with PROOF-2a, change the plan. Think about what wires actually are: the wiring diagram is an adjacency matrix; what is sent over the wires is another story; defining the matrix and proving that SOMETHING is sent over each wire is simple.** So the criteria below are read in two layers: the MATRIX (which box sends to which; `spike/wm_adjacency.bb` generates it from the map at a named commit as `wm-adjacency.edn` and draws it as `wm-adjacency.svg`, futon3c 5cc3bc8c) and the CONTENT (what is sent), and a wire is verified at the first layer when a registered test shows something is sent: the reader receives, under the field, a non-absent value the writer produced, pinned from a live record. What the value means is the content layer, later. At ad3afb76 the matrix has 75 boxes, 93 wires in 73 entries, 15 weakly connected components among the 53 non-test boxes (one of 24, the loop; fourteen of size 1 to 3), two isolated boxes (`:r4-coapply`, `:r11-warrants`), and the six step boundaries from loop entry to selection crossed by no wire.

**What "complete" means, checkable.** The map is complete when (1) no boundary between adjacent steps is crossed by no declared field (the harness figure's hand-off strip has no red chevron) and the carriers that hand data across them are boxes; (2) every edge of the equation DAG (futon-2026 Figure 6A, regenerated from the current registry) has a counterpart in the map, so the map is a strict superset of 6A and the join `spike/wm_vs_equation_dag.bb` reports 0 unboxed at var grain; (3) every entry of the matrix has a wire test at the first layer: a registered test that pins, from a live record, that something is sent, the reader's value under the field non-absent and the writer's; what is sent is the content layer, after; (4) the organisation layer is drawn: the step order, the carriers (`flight/run!`, `run-phase!`, `judge-opts`, `target-view`, the tick's opts) and which box calls which, from the source. Flights resume when all four hold, and the first flight after is a test of the map, not a search for defects.

**JOE (11:58Z), three corrections and a requisition.** (a) "In this context, I don't draw things. I requisition things": the server is in Amsterdam, Joe in Dallas without a graphical window manager; a DAG generator that cannot accept new nodes while the DAG is incomplete is to be replaced: an updated program, and a new up-to-date map of the dependencies in the Lean model. Requisitioned: WM-DAG-NODES-I (claude-2: nodes from data through the amendments mechanism `gen_control_stages.py` already carries, R19 mission preference and the grain gate as a support restriction declared, Figure 6A regenerated, edges still 31) and WM-LEAN-DEP-MAP-I (claude-11: `gen_lean_dag.bb`, the 203 modules of `mathlib4/DarkTower/WarMachine` at module and declaration grain, joined to the registry's `:lean` fields, outputs beside Figure 6A); WM-EQUATIONS-APPLY-I (claude-3 applies its delta, 37 edges) follows the nodes packet. claude-8's error on the record: "get a box only if Joe draws one" treated a hand-drawn input as fixed; the input is the thing to replace. (b) The legacy habit store's writer (`full_loop_runner.clj:4603-4608`) is to be RETIRED, the file kept as history: WM-HABIT-STORE-RETIRE-I, queued for the runner file after WM-CHOSEN-CANDIDATE-I returns (shared working tree, one editor of that file at a time). (c) On "click-to-flight recordkeeping": Joe's terms: a CLICK is a run of the machine; it TRIGGERS a flight if it produces a cascade, a typed hole, a wiring diagram and an implementation of the wiring diagram; multiple clicks per flight would mean stopping mid-flight, which is not an operating plan. The draft's definition (PROOF-2a line 54, "A flight: the clicks on one mission, ending in closure") and the code (`flight/run!` loops clicks on one target up to `max-clicks`) say the opposite containment. Open for Joe's ruling, put in the report; WM-FLIGHT-RECORD-PER-CLICK-I is HELD, since under Joe's terms the gap it fixes does not exist. JOE (12:13Z): NOT a ruling, an empirical question: the sweet spot preserves capability without overhead; there is no proof of functioning yet, much less of capability, so a ruling is premature. A run may create many records and do much inference and never do any work (the state of the art, sadly), or be efficient at finding valuable work and doing it; whether the machine keeps working end-to-end on one problem to finish it or jumps around the stack doing useful work is not what matters now; Joe's own preference, recorded in his notes on C, is focused work that leads to completion; whether that breaks down across reselection of an adjacent focus in a series of related tasks or head-down work on one thing is settled IN A GIVEN INSTANCE, not in general. claude-8: so the draft's definition stands with a note, not a change: the number of clicks a flight spends on one target is a per-instance parameter, and the code already has it as one (`flight/run!`'s `max-clicks`); the click/flight vocabulary is not amended. WM-FLIGHT-RECORD-PER-CLICK-I stays held and becomes CONDITIONAL: dispatched only when an instance chooses more than one click per flight, since until then it is overhead on a machine not yet shown to function.

**PROOF-2b?** claude-8's recommendation, for Joe's decision: no new proof. The theorem is unchanged; what changed is the plan's premise (components ready, wiring only) and that is a plan amendment, recorded in PROOF-2a's "Work this opens" as the governing order, with this phase as its instrument. A PROOF-2b would restate the same theorem under a corrected plan.

## MAP (discovery in flight)

Joe, 2026-09-25 ~18:40Z: "we should look into whether we have any *good* patterns or prior work that can help with the wiring of components. I know we have done some wiring diagrams in futon5, so we may be able to reuse those ideas." Sources located by claude-8 and handed to claude-11 for the MAP discovery (MAP-WIRING-D, read-only): futon5's wiring vocabulary and design principles (`docs/chapter0-aif-as-wiring-diagram.md`, `docs/ct-wiring-vocabulary.md`, `docs/wiring-design-principles-from-good-bands.md`, M-fulab-wiring-survey, the wiring-ladder data and compare scripts); M-futon-seams's token-flow wiring diagrams derived from cascades with a hand overlay and checked for orphans (`holes/labs/M-futon-seams/wiring/*.edn`, `wiring_from_cascade.py`, `wiring_check.py`); the lifecycle's rule that a wiring diagram is required on any of four triggers (mission-lifecycle.md L398-425; this mission meets all four: it is a machine, several parties write fields others read, fields cross the Clojure/Lean/bb boundaries, and its claims rest on the flight's own record); library patterns `process/built-but-not-wired-invisibility`, `test-registry/warrant-only-the-wires`, `workshop/honest-holes-gate-composed-claims`, `workshop/COMPOSITOR.md`, and the apparatus set. The discovery answers the lifecycle's Q1-Q5 for this mission and recommends the diagram form; the diagram itself is DERIVE's.

**MAP-WIRING-D landed** (futon2 `proof2/packets/MAP-WIRING-D.md`, 7cc3fa82, claude-11, read-only; pending the owner's read).

*Q1, what exists.* The best reusable prior work is not in futon5: it is `futon3c.diagramprover.wiring` (futon3c c474470f), a checker that takes a map of components, each naming its source file and the record fields it reads and writes, and reports fields written but never read, read but never written, multiply written, and mismatches between a component's declaration and its named file (`ingest`, `written-never-read`, `read-never-written`, `multiply-written`, `conformance`, `phase-chain-findings`); a worked map already covers a futon2 War Machine path (`holes/labs/M-diagramprover/wm-wiring.edn`, claude-7, 2026-08-15). futon5's wiring files are almost all cellular-automaton wiring (rules from bit primitives); the exception is `src/futon5/ct/mission.clj` (aeeee96), validators for a component diagram (completeness, coverage, no orphan inputs, type safety, and the I1-I6 invariants of `docs/chapter0-aif-as-wiring-diagram.md`, whose I4 exogeneity test at 207-209 asks whether a path from any Action to any Preference bypasses the Environment); futon5 has no CLAUDE.md, and the "source material, not running infrastructure" phrase is futon3c's about futon3. The wiring-ladder's add-one-component-at-a-time pattern (`data/wiring-ladder/README.md:5-10`) transfers. M-futon-seams's token-flow diagrams show one target's cascade of work, not the machine that flies it. Library: `built-but-not-wired-invisibility` ("no per-component test can catch it; only a rung that refuses on unconsumed machinery can"; keep an inventory of unwired components), `warrant-only-the-wires` (a field contract between endpoints is a wire: one artifact, its sha pinned by both endpoint warrants), `detection-wired-to-single-catastrophic-response` (the same shape as E-outer-loop O3), `honest-holes-gate-composed-claims`, COMPOSITOR C3 and C6, and the apparatus set (`done-is-observed-running`, `one-authority-per-question`, `loudness-is-conserved`, …).

*Prior rounds.* futon5a `M-war-machine-wiring` (VERIFY, 2026-05-01, 1,848 lines) stopped at this exact gap, "Both endpoints now exist; the connector doesn't", with INSTANTIATE deferred; futon2 `M-G-wm-wiring` (HEAD) was never worked, every checkpoint unticked. DERIVE reads both.

*Q3, readiness per row.* The flight is `flight/run!` (flight.clj:246), production caller `flight_driver.clj:123` under `--run`; each click is a POST, so rows 4, 6 and 9 happen inside `war_machine.clj`. Row 0: no enactment-writing code; both click-001 record files are hand-authored. Rows 5, 7: no caller anywhere. Row 6: `sourced-rates` called with nil labels (war_machine.clj:5889), no `:measurement`. Row 4: the kernel reads `:precedence` (efe.clj:1163), nothing reads `:order`. Row 9: `:selection-law` carries no candidate id. Row 2: the extractor has no `ns`; `served_by_reading.clj` does not exist (claude-10's packet in flight). Row 3: partly ready, no library root passed. Rows 1, 8, 10: the code the rows call is missing. Row 11: done.

*Q4, the form.* The futon3c component map, because its mismatch check reads the real source files; the other two forms are checked only against themselves. Keep the token-flow form for M-autoclock-in's own cascade; take futon5's I4 as one extra question of the map. On today's code the checks would flag: the grain and enactment fields rows 5 and 7 read but nothing writes; `:measurement` and `:order` declared but absent; selection reading the legacy habit store rather than the fold's output.

*Q5, what the method cannot check.* A component left out of the map is invisible to every check; the mismatch check is a text match, so a field named only in a comment passes; no red-tape rate; the refusals a flight can reach (now REFUSAL-REGISTER-D); step-outcome rates absent before any flight; fields crossing the HTTP boundary cannot be matched to one source file.

*Triggers.* All four hold; a wiring diagram is required.

**Joe's read (2026-09-25 ~20:40Z): MAP exit met.** "I looked at it, I like the idea of using the diagramprover to prove that the map does what it is supposed to do. We have prior proofs for apm-capability but I think no such proof for a war machine capability. It would be a nice complement to the PROOF-2a we're working on." So the map is not only DERIVE's diagram but the subject of a **capability proof by construction** in the form of `futon3c/holes/labs/M-diagramprover/capability-proof.md` (a TOP claim decomposed into numbered nodes, each upgraded only with a machine-checkable warrant; the APM note `capability-proof-apm.tex` is the worked instance, its N9 "the pipeline runs continuously" the constructive content). A note on the record: `capability-proof-war-machine.tex` v0 exists (8 August 2026, futon3c 7eede051), but its subject is the War Machine's *open-source programming* capability with a maintainer merge as the grader; the proof Joe asks for here is of the *wiring*: that the map's declared reads and writes hold against the real files (the diagramprover's conformance checks), so that the machine PROOF-2a's clauses describe is the machine that runs. Complement to PROOF-2a: PROOF-2a proves the flight is AIF under Clauses C, D, E, T on the record; the wiring proof shows each clause's fields are written by one authority and read by their consumer in the code that flies.

**Owner's answer on the proof (claude-10, ~20:55Z).** Decompose by CLAUSE, rows as sub-nodes: a node is a claim, and the claims are the clauses (row 5 serves C through G_c; rows 4 and 9 both touch the selection record; nodes by row would make the proof's shape follow the order the work was done). TOP: "the flight of a lifecycle mission runs the machine PROOF-2a's clauses describe: each clause's fields are written by one authority and read by their consumer in the code that flies." Nodes: **N0 referent** (the map's sites are the code that flies: each site resolves to a namespace in the flight's recorded load identity, so the map describes the machine that runs and not a second one; `wm-wiring.edn`'s header records how close the paper map came to mapping the pilot instead); **N-T** (rows 1, 8); **N-C** (rows 2, 5, 9, 0); **N-D** (row 4); **N-A** (row 6); **N-E** (row 7); **N-publish** (row 10); **N-run**, the constructive content and counterpart of APM's N9: a flight of a lifecycle mission produces a click record with every clause's fields filled or typed-absent, warranted by that flight's record plus the W checkers run over it, which conformance cannot supply. For a checker run to be a node's warrant the map's EDN carries: expected findings declared PER UNWIRED ROW (`:expected-findings [{:finding :read-never-written :field :grain}]`) with the registered namespace asserting the checker's report equals the declared set exactly (zero findings on wired rows alone would pass a row whose findings vanished because the keyword turned up in a comment; with set equality a change in either direction fails, and wiring a row means deleting its expected findings in the same commit as the code); each site the file sha it was drawn against, so the certificate names the bytes it checked; a test box per row; distinctive keys. Two checker additions as a packet on M-diagramprover's side, in order: (1) var-grain sites `{:file … :var "defn-name"}` scanning only that top-level form (without it conformance on the 7,800-line war_machine.clj means little, and rows 4, 6, 9 live there); (2) a textual read/write distinction (write = assoc/assoc-in/map-literal key position; read = get/get-in/keyword-as-function/destructuring), marked heuristic as the checker's docstring already is; and N0's check, that a site resolves on a given load closure. The v0 note skeleton is the owner's, written with the map (TOP, the nodes, a warrant-state table, and where the proof stops: conformance is textual; N-run needs a flight that has not happened; Clause T's selection is open until the outer cascade is decided), stated definitively with dated measurements per Joe's rule for papers, claude-8 reviewing. Order: the map, then the checker packet, then the skeleton. CHECKER ADDITIONS LANDED (DIAGRAMPROVER-WIRING-I, claude-11, futon3c 0965b80a var-grain sites, 6f4490a5 read/write heuristic, ca4c2e7f load-closure check; warrant test-registry-f24589cc pinned at ca4c2e7f, wiring-test 32/63; scope graph.clj, wiring.clj, wiring_test.clj, the two fixture files outside it; nothing loaded into :7070): (1) a site `{:file|:ns … :var "name"}` scans only the first top-level list whose second element is that symbol, found by a paren-aware text scan `parse-forms` (strings, regexes, comments, `#_`, char literals, `^meta`, reader prefixes, `::alias/k`, reader conditionals; never evaluates), `:var-not-found` when no such form, sites without `:var` scanned as before; the control `wm-wiring.edn` report pinned from the checker at c474470f over futon2 files copied from git at e1f6d909 into a temp root (so futon2 moving on does not move the control), and since the map's own report is `[]` a perturbed version with one absent write and one undeclared reader (2 findings) is pinned beside it, both unchanged after the three commits. (2) `field-usage` gives `{:reads :writes :unclassified}` per field by the listed positions, plus keys in get-in/assoc-in/update-in paths and threading steps; ONE DEPARTURE from the packet, claude-11's call: the findings `:declared-write-not-found` / `:declared-read-not-found` (with counts and `:heuristic true`) appear only under `{:heuristic? true}`, off by default, since on by default they would change the control's report and break three exact-equality tests, against the packet's own byte-identical requirement; run on the control with the heuristic on, 5 findings: `:likelihood-mode` is READ by belief.clj (a :keys destructuring at :400) and WRITTEN by war_machine.clj into a map literal (:834, :1045) while the map declares the opposite direction (drift in the worked map, or the map describing the value at another level; undecided, for the reviewer), and the three hashes in the live-wiring test occur only inside a set literal, `:unclassified`. (3) `load-closure-findings` / `sites-resolve?` take the registry's `:load-closure` ({:ns :path :sha256} from `closure-from-entries`) or a plain path list, with the map's repo root as first argument since the registry records paths relative to the registered repo; `:site-not-in-load-closure`; pure. Planted bugs each caught (var ignored 7 failures; the prefix guard dropped 1; everything-a-write 8; closure check always empty 4); `:g` vs `:g-terms` holds at var grain. claude-8's bad case in futon3c's own process, through `conformance` with a `{:file :var}` site: a form carrying the field only inside a nested fn literal gives no finding, and the neighbouring form gives one, so the scan is the whole top-level form (a first attempt against the parser's internals errored on claude-8's own wrong call shape, not on the checker; recorded so the run count is honest). REVIEW (claude-10, ~21:20Z): ACCEPTED; the tests pin what was asked and the map is checkable with them; claude-10 also checked that a declared field occurring only in unclassified positions still yields a finding (wiring.clj:379), so an occurrence the heuristic cannot classify cannot satisfy a declaration. For the map: rows 4, 6, 9 get var-grain sites in war_machine.clj, their reads are keyword-as-function or :keys destructuring and `:measurement` a map-literal key, all classified; N0 uses `sites-resolve?` against the flight's `:load-closure`. Heuristic-off default ACCEPTED (the map's registered namespace passes `{:heuristic? true}` explicitly and declares its expected findings under it), with one docstring line asked for: "direction checked only with :heuristic? true". `:likelihood-mode` is DRIFT IN THE WORKED MAP, not a level question: at e1f6d909 war_machine.clj decides the mode (`arena-likelihood-mode` :818) and writes it into the options map (:834, :1045), belief.clj only reads it (:keys with :or at :400, case at :406) and writes it nowhere; the map's comment claims belief-model stamps the mode with the A/B/D hashes, a provenance write the code does not make. Fix on M-diagramprover's side (DIAGRAMPROVER-WIRING-I2, claude-11): `:war-machine-report` writes and `:belief-model` reads; drop the stamps claim for the mode; re-pin the heuristic report; the three hashes' `:unclassified` inside a set literal is a correct limit of the heuristic, an example for the docstring, not a map change. Default controls unchanged ([] and the perturbed two; the graph checks []); the heuristic report over the corrected map is pinned with a new assertion, its only findings the three expected `:declared-read-not-found` on the live-wiring test's set-literal hashes; the uncorrected map had given the two `:likelihood-mode` findings, so the new assertion would fail on it (seen before the fix, not re-run against the old map, stated as such).

**Owner's read (claude-10, 2026-09-25 ~19:25Z): MAP exit met on the owner's side.** The component-map form is accepted. DERIVE's first artefact is the map for the M-autoclock-in flight, drawn from the inventory rows, checked against futon2's real files, its findings the wiring's to-do list; claude-10 draws it under carve-out (b), claude-8 reviews. One weakness in the checker shapes the drawing: `conformance` (futon3c wiring.clj:101-105) is a textual keyword search over the whole site file, comments included, and does not tell a read from a write, so against the 6,000-line `war_machine.clj` a declared read of `:order` or `:measurement` would pass if the keyword appeared anywhere, the green-but-vacuous shape. So: tick fields are declared by their distinctive record keys (`:measurement`, `:precedence-violations`, the `:selection-law` candidate key) not generic ones; each row gets a second box, its own test namespace, declared as the reader (as `wm-wiring.edn` already does with `a_matrix_live_wiring_test`), and a row counts as wired only when its test box conforms; tick rows 4, 6 and 9 are listed as findings with line numbers rather than trusted to the file-level check.

*Q3 amended by the owner.* Rows 4, 6 and 9 are changes to `war_machine.clj` (and `efe.clj` for row 4), not the flight; each its own commit with its own test. Row 6: passing admitted labels at :5889 changes no numbers today, since no class-level admitted labels exist and every class will read `:measurement :absent`; the value is that the field appears on the record, which stops a zero kernel reading as an exact observation, and its test pins exactly that. Row 4 is a change in behaviour, not only wiring: reading `:order` changes G on any click whose containment is not a chain; two cases: a chain with empty `:precedence-violations` gives G equal to the list kernel's exactly; a non-chain may differ, recorded against Lean 69c2. Row 9: `:per-policy-argmax` is already on `:selection-law` (war_machine.clj:4619), so the candidate id is probably derivable in the tick with no new data, the smallest of the three. **Tick order: 9, then 6, then 4** (most additive to the one that changes G). **Flight order: 0 → 5 → the W_c call from the flight → 7** (the `increment` pass-through is already at 531cfaaa), in parallel with the tick rows. Row 1 stays last, after OUTER-CASCADE-D is decided; row 8 goes with row 1.

## DERIVE

Joe, 2026-09-25 ~21:35Z: "the mission lifecycle would say that DERIVE is next, though we may have already done that step." Most of it was derived in the record as owner's decisions (claude-10) and reviews; this section gathers them under the lifecycle's headings and names what is still open. Nothing here is new design; every item cites where it was decided.

**Entity types.** The *components* (inventory rows 0-11: a namespace or script at a sha, with a warrant). The *click record* (the shape of `holes/labs/M-futon-seams/exemplar/click-001.edn`) and the *enactment record* (`click-001-enactment.edn`: attempts, checks, grain, deviations), both hand-authored today; row 0 makes the flight write the second. The *field entry* (`target_field.clj`: `:next-step`, `:requisition`, `:eligible`, `:ineligible-reason`). The *requisition line* under an excursion's H1 (in-progress, completed), a different semantic layer from a pending E-/M-/T- job. The *proposal* and the *link* (served-by reading: quotes → spans → `verify-proposed-link` → a link with `:basis`, or a refusal with its reason). The *component map* (diagramprover EDN: boxes with sites `{:file|:ns :var}`, declared reads and writes, per-row test boxes, `:expected-findings` per unwired row, file shas). The *proof nodes* (N0, N-T, N-C, N-D, N-A, N-E, N-publish, N-run). The *refusal register* rows (kind, site, class, case).

**Relation types.** Box *writes* field, box *reads* field (the map; checked by `written-never-read`, `read-never-written`, `multiply-written`, `conformance`). Row *serves* clause (rows 2, 5, 9, 0 → C; 4 → D; 6 → A; 7 → E; 1, 8 → T; 10 → publish). Node *warranted-by* (a test-registry warrant, or the flight's record plus the W checkers for N-run). Requisition *makes-ineligible* target. Proposal *verified-as* link, or *refused-with* reason. Refusal *has-case* (a ruling, packet or site comment by file:line) or *typed-absence*.

**Invariant rules** (checkable).
1. One authority per field: no field on the click or enactment record has two writers (`multiply-written` = [] over the map).
2. Every declared read has a writer and every declared write a reader (`read-never-written` = `written-never-read` = [] over the map on wired rows).
3. The checker's report over the map equals the declared `:expected-findings` exactly (set equality; wiring a row deletes its expected findings in the same commit as the code).
4. Every site resolves in the flight's recorded load closure (`sites-resolve?`, N0).
5. An absence is typed on the record and never read as a value (the whole PROOF-2a discipline; the nonfinite-G and requisition work are this rule applied).
6. No refusal without a case: every refusal a flight can reach is in the register with its case, or is decided when a flight reaches it; a refusal reached off the list is a wiring defect (IDENTIFY 4(a)).
7. A flight's record is accepted whether the flight succeeded or failed, when accurate.
8. The witness conditions W₀..Wₜ are checkable on every click record the wiring produces (PROOF-2a).

**Data flow.** `flight/run!` (flight.clj:246; production caller `flight_driver.clj:123` under `--run`) → read step: `extract-outcomes` (v5) beside the wants, seats' quotes placed by `served-by-reading`, links verified by `verify-proposed-link` with the text's sha, proposals and refusals recorded → ask step: `wi/prompt` with the library root, `validate-response` on the reply, publication landing inside the click (row 3's test pins both halves) → click: a POST into `war_machine.clj` (assembly, constructor, scoring, selection, gate, judge; every refusal an abstention on the click record) → click record (`:selection-law` with the candidate id, row 9; `:measurement` from `sourced-rates` with admitted labels, row 6; the kernel reading `:order` when `:precedence-violations` is empty, row 4) → enactment record written by row 0's step (the grain gate's pass or typed refusal before the grain attempt is committed, row 5; the W_c checker shelled out to `bb … --wc`, its verdict into `enactment-habit/increment` unchanged, row 7) → the habit fold, read by selection (row 7) → the outer cascade (row 1): the action set over eligible field entries (cascade-real facts, the target field's next steps, the operator-turn evidence), the mixture law's draw with its seed on the record, pair overlaps (row 8), a tick's observation (row 10).

**IF / HOWEVER / THEN / BECAUSE** (the settled decisions, in the lifecycle's form).
- IF the wiring needs a diagram, HOWEVER futon5's wiring is cellular-automaton wiring and the token-flow form shows one target's cascade not the machine, THEN the diagram is a diagramprover component map, BECAUSE its conformance check reads the real source files where the other forms are checked only against themselves (MAP, claude-11; owner's read ~19:25Z).
- IF the checker's conformance is a whole-file keyword search, HOWEVER war_machine.clj is 7,800 lines and three rows live in it, THEN sites are var-grain and keys distinctive and each row has a test box as the declared reader, BECAUSE a file-level match against that file is the green-but-vacuous shape (claude-10 ~19:25Z; DIAGRAMPROVER-WIRING-I).
- IF a checker run is to be a node's warrant, HOWEVER zero findings on wired rows would pass a row whose findings vanished into a comment, THEN the map declares expected findings per unwired row and the test asserts set equality, BECAUSE a change in either direction must fail (claude-10 ~20:55Z).
- IF the proof needs nodes, HOWEVER rows are units of work and the claims are the clauses, THEN the proof is decomposed by clause with rows as sub-nodes plus N0 and N-run, BECAUSE a node should be a claim (claude-10 ~20:55Z).
- IF direction findings are useful, HOWEVER on by default they would change the pinned control, THEN the heuristic is behind an explicit flag and the docstring says direction is checked only under it, BECAUSE the control stays byte-identical (claude-11; accepted ~21:20Z).
- IF the eleven tick refusals have no case, HOWEVER none is on the first flight's path and each already reaches the record as an abstention, THEN they are decided when a flight reaches them, BECAUSE reviewing code the first flight cannot reach is the busy work Joe struck (claude-10 ~20:10Z).
- IF Kimi requisition files are in the field, HOWEVER a requisition is a different semantic layer from a pending job, THEN eligibility is read from the requisition line and ineligible entries stay on the record with their reason, BECAUSE creation makes a pending object eligible and a requisition flags its work in-progress then completed (Joe ~17:40Z, ~18:05Z).
- IF no target has a G, HOWEVER dropping targets without one would exclude for lack of machine work, THEN the selection law is the mixture (E outside D, the posterior within D scaled by E(D)) with the draw's seed on the record, BECAUSE every written mission is feasible and a draw under uniform E is uncheckable without its seed. PROPOSED (claude-10, futon2 d615d06e), not decided: the direction is pending Joe's read of OUTER-CASCADE-D (owner's correction, ~21:50Z).
- IF a candidate's G is nonfinite, HOWEVER a throw loses every candidate, THEN it is a typed absence on that candidate and left out of the comparison, and a nonfinite baseline is its own absence, BECAUSE a NaN in the min comparison picks an arbitrary winner and no ΔG exists against an undefined baseline (claude-10 ~19:10Z, ~19:55Z).
- IF the tick order could follow the rows, HOWEVER row 4 changes G and row 9 adds only data, THEN the tick order is 9, 6, 4 and the flight order 0 → 5 → the W_c call → claude-13's `increment` fix (passing the `:join-unverifiable` status through; a component change, so before row 7's wiring, not inside it; on the record it landed at futon2 531cfaaa, test `join-unverifiable-verdict-keeps-its-status`, so the step is done) → 7, BECAUSE the most additive change goes first (claude-10 ~19:25Z; order corrected by the owner ~21:50Z).

**View / UI.** The mission's working page in the M-futon-seams presentation (generator parameterised, futon3c dc86c962): the mission as main column, margin notes per row (the component called, with sha and warrant; the PROOF-2a clause exercised), the phase table read from exit criteria, the map and its checker report in the figure lane. The diagramprover report itself (findings as EDN). The proof note (TOP, nodes, warrant-state table, where the proof stops).

**Wiring diagram (required: all four triggers).** OPEN. Joe, ~22:10Z: "this is just the example, I was assuming what we would DERIVE is the actual wiring for the WM itself; there's an interesting self-similarity here for sure." So the diagram is the MACHINE'S map, and the M-autoclock-in flight is its first trace (the witness N-run needs), not the map's subject. The inventory rows are already the machine's components (outer selection 1, 8; the flight 0, 2, 3, 5, 7; the tick 4, 6, 9; publish 10), so what the machine's map adds is the loop's closure, the boxes that make it run without a hand-placed target: the scheduled tick entry (`full_loop_runner` / the scheduled run) → the outer cascade's selection over the eligible field (row 1) → clocking the chosen target into a lane (M-autoclock-in as the mechanism, NOTE-task-manager-outer-loop) → `flight/run!` → the flight's end feeding back: the enactment record into the habit fold (row 7) and E; requisition state into eligibility (`target_field`); the observation of publish (row 10) → the next selection. The self-similarity, carried as a tension rather than settled: the outer cascade chooses (target, step) as the inner chooses (pattern, attempt); a flight's enactment record is the outer cascade's observation; M-wm-wiring is itself a lifecycle mission and so a target of the machine it wires, and a flight of it by the machine would produce, as its record, the machine's own wiring at N-run's grain. The map for the M-autoclock-in flight (WM-MAP-I) becomes the machine's map with the loop-closure boxes added and that flight marked as the first trace through it.

**DRAWN (WM-MAP-I, futon3c 3e96fdce, claude-10; warrant test-registry-5cdd0298, `futon3c.diagramprover.wm-flight-wiring-test` 4/9; claude-8 review: three paths on master, the map's 23 findings counted, the test pins the map's sha256 and futon2 dd1c0561 and passes `{:heuristic? true}` explicitly; first run of the new namespace by claude-8 in futon3c's own JVM: 4 tests, 9 assertions, green).** `holes/labs/M-wm-wiring/wm-flight-wiring.edn`: one box per row 0-11 plus a test box per row as declared reader; sites at var grain against futon2 dd1c0561, materialised from git into a temp root by the test; each box declares what the WIRED code will read and write, so where the code does not yet, the checker's finding is that row's to-do; keys chosen distinctive at their site (row 2 uses `:want-span` and `:text-sha256`, `:basis` occurring at four other sites); the header records the referent and how the earlier map nearly mapped the pilot; the W_c checker is siteless (a futon3c script). Test: the report (structural checks plus conformance under the flag) equals the declared expected findings exactly; `multiply-written` []; the pins. Mutation: dropping one expected finding fails the equality (naming `:var-not-found observe-publication-fn`) and the pin; with nothing wired the test passes.

**THE WIRING TO-DO LIST = the 20 expected findings that wiring removes (each deleted from the map in the same commit as its code):**

### PSR — Pattern Selection Records (DERIVE, claude-10, 2026-09-25)

Each selection appears in the map (`holes/labs/M-wm-wiring/wm-flight-wiring.edn`, futon3c 3e96fdce) or the fidelity matrix (`fidelity-matrix.edn`) as a concrete feature; none is a gate.

#### PSR-1: `process/built-but-not-wired-invisibility` for the unwired components

- Pattern chosen: process/built-but-not-wired-invisibility
- Candidates: built-but-not-wired-invisibility, a prose inventory table only
- Rationale: Eleven components were closed singly and none is called from the tick or the flight; a binary "closed" reads them as present. The map's `:expected-findings` (20 `:to-do`) are the inventory, checked against futon2's files: a component stays listed until the code that consumes it exists, and wiring a row deletes its entries in the same commit.
- Trace in the map: `:expected-findings`, `:kind :to-do`.
- Confidence: high

#### PSR-2: `test-registry/warrant-only-the-wires` for the row tests

- Pattern chosen: test-registry/warrant-only-the-wires
- Candidates: warrant-only-the-wires, one integration test over the whole flight
- Rationale: Each row's warrant should name the wire it proves (a field written at one site and read at another), not general behaviour. Every row has a test box as its declared reader; the matrix names the same namespace as that row's tripwire.
- Trace in the map: the `:box/kind :test` box per row; in the matrix, `:tripwires` named by those namespaces.
- Confidence: high

#### PSR-3: `apparatus/one-authority-per-question` for record fields

- Pattern chosen: apparatus/one-authority-per-question
- Candidates: one-authority-per-question, allow duplicate writers that agree
- Rationale: A field on the click or enactment record with two writers can agree today and diverge later with no finding. Invariant 1: every field has one writer.
- Trace in the map: `multiply-written` = [] asserted by `futon3c.diagramprover.wm-flight-wiring-test`.
- Confidence: high

#### PSR-4: `apparatus/done-is-observed-running` for the machine as a whole

- Pattern chosen: apparatus/done-is-observed-running
- Candidates: done-is-observed-running, declaring the wiring done when every row's test passes
- Rationale: Rows that pass their tests singly do not show the machine acting. The machine exists when a flight's record shows each box of the trace acting: N-run, whose witness is the first trace (M-autoclock-in) matched step by step to the map's boxes.
- Trace in the map: N-run and the `:traces` field (next map commit); load-closure resolution (N0) as its precondition.
- Confidence: medium — the trace check is designed, not yet built.

## ARGUE

Joe, 2026-09-25 ~23:15Z: "we should supply it. You said 'the outer cascade, the enactment step, and the flight's checker call' are missing. So ARGUE should also explain how those will be supplied." Drafted by claude-8 from the record; pending the owner's read (claude-10) and Joe's.

**Why this design and not another.** The gap is that every component exists and nothing calls it, and that earlier rounds spent their time on refusals whose contracts were never specified. The design answers both with one object: a map that declares, per component, which record fields it reads and writes, checked against the real source by a checker that already exists, with its remaining findings declared exactly. Wiring then has one meaning, a declared read or write that the code now performs, and one test, the finding leaving the map in the same commit as the code. Red tape has one meaning too: a refusal on the register without a case, and the register is complete for the path a first flight takes up to the click (flight and tick); a flight that enacts reaches a chosen action, and the post-choice register (claude-3, on first reach) precedes the first enacting flight (owner's correction, ~23:35Z). Nothing in the design is a new contract; every field it names is one PROOF-2a's clauses already require on the record.

**How the three unbuilt boxes are supplied.** These are the gaps on the exemplar trace (`:r0-enact-step`, `:r7-flight-call`, `:r1-outer-cascade`), the only places on the M-autoclock-in path where the machine's code does not exist. Each is supplied by one row of the inventory, in this order, and each already has its consumer and its producer on the map, so what is built is the hop between them and nothing more.

1. **The enactment step (row 0), then the grain gate (row 5).** `flight/run!` (flight.clj:246) takes its steps as functions: `read-fn`, `ask-fn`, `observe-fn`, `click-fn`. The enactment step is one more, `enact-fn` in `flight_runner.clj` (the map's `:intended-site`), called after the click with the click's decision. An enactment record lists attempts, each a change someone made and checked; for click-001 the owner made them by hand. So `enact-fn` DISPATCHES the chosen candidate's pattern steps to a seat, as the ask step already dispatches through `agency-answer-fn`, collects each commit, runs each attempt's check, and writes the attempt, in the shape of `click-001-enactment.edn` (attempts, checks, grain, deviations), which the W_c checker already reads. It fetches the click's run record as well (`record-summary` already reads it), since `check-c` takes the click record and the enactment and on the flight's path the click record lives server-side. Marking the grain attempt belongs to row 0, being part of the record's shape: the mark comes from the candidate, the attempt at the pattern whose interpretation declares `:grain`; no candidate declares one today, so row 0 records `{:grain-attempt {:absent :candidate-names-no-grain-pattern}}` and the gate (row 5) then refuses `:grain-not-declared` with its own reason, recorded, the flight continuing. Before the grain attempt's commit it calls `futon2.aif.grain-gate` and records the gate's pass or typed refusal on the attempt (row 5). `enact-fn` dispatches each pattern step to a seat and records the result; the post-choice register precedes the first enacting flight. Supplied by: one packet on `flight_runner.clj` with `flight_enact_test` as its test box, then one on the gate call with `flight_grain_gate_test`; the map's findings `enact-fn` `:not-built` and the two test files go in those commits. Why it is first: rows 5, 7 and 9 write into the record it creates.
2. **The flight's checker call (before row 7).** The W_c checker is `proof2a_check.clj`, a bb script in futon3c that re-runs the grain gate and reads the selection law's candidate id (4bc95005). It stays the one checker: the flight shells out to `bb … --wc` with EDN output on the enactment record it just wrote, and hands the verdict, a vector of failures, `[]`, or the typed `{:status :join-unverifiable}`, to `enactment-habit/increment` unchanged (the pass-through is at 531cfaaa). The fold then holds the first machine-produced enactment, and row 7 makes selection read `:enactment-records` from the fold instead of the legacy habit store, which the matrix lists as the one drop. Supplied by: its OWN packet before row 7 (a separate box on the map, `:r7-flight-call`, with its own assertion: the verdict arrives as data, identical to the checker's own output), preceded by one small change to the checker, which is the owner's file: `proof2a_check.clj --wc` prints lines of text today, so the packet adds an `--edn` output mode; both files, the enactment and the click's run record, are passed to it; the `:join-unverifiable` pass-through in `increment` (531cfaaa) is a claim the owner verifies by reading that diff when row 7 opens; then row 7 on `policy/select-action-cascades` with `selection_reads_fold_test`. Why here: E must come from enactments the machine made, or the habit prior is a value standing in for an absence.
3. **The outer cascade (row 1), last.** `src/futon2/aif/outer_cascade.clj` `select`: the action set is every eligible field entry paired with its own `:next-step`, plus `defer` and `learn`; its facts are the field's, cascade-real's per-mission facts, and the operator-turn evidence typed by kind; its rates per step kind are read through `measured-cell` and stay typed absent until flights have produced counts; the choice is the mixture law, every target keeping its E mass, targets with a ΔG re-weighted among themselves, reducing to a seeded draw from E when no target has a G, with the draw's generator, seed and value on the record so the choice can be recomputed. It writes `:chosen-target` and `:draw-seed`; the flight entry reads `:chosen-target` instead of `--target`; the scheduled entry starts a flight instead of a tick. Supplied by: one packet after Joe decides the direction (OUTER-CASCADE-D and claude-10's read are the two readings), with `outer_cascade_test` and `loop_closure_test`; row 8's pair overlaps go with it. Why last: rows 0-10 can be wired and flown against the hand-placed target without it (c2061e27), and its data is not yet in Cascade Live. DIRECTION DECIDED (Joe, ~23:45Z): "the outer cascade should be based on Cascade Live. You found that Cascade Live itself is missing some data but in any case this gives us a direction to go in." So row 1's supply is: first the data Cascade Live lacks for the field (excursion and ticket nodes so the join covers the 282 targets that cannot match by construction; the mission→pattern section readable, 1,173 edges over 223 missions by direct read today; lineage carrying `:dispatched-by`), sized by CASCADE-LIVE-GAPS-D (claude-12) and built on Cascade Live's side; then `outer_cascade.clj select` as above, with the composition (the cascade supplies the actions, the mixture law the choice, the seed on the record) as the working definition.

**Pattern cross-reference** (from the four PSRs and MAP): `process/built-but-not-wired-invisibility` applies at the map's `:expected-findings` (the inventory of unwired components, and the rung that refuses on unconsumed machinery); `test-registry/warrant-only-the-wires` at the test box per row and the field contracts as wires pinned by both endpoint warrants; `apparatus/one-authority-per-question` at `multiply-written` = []; `apparatus/done-is-observed-running` at N-run and the trace; `process/detection-wired-to-single-catastrophic-response` at the refusal register's three classes (a tick refusal is an abstention the flight continues past, never a stop); `aif/grounded-actuation-not-reobservation` at the enactment record as the witness the fold reads; `futon-theory/mission-interface-signature` at the map itself. None revises DERIVE.

**Theoretical coherence.** IDENTIFY's anchoring holds: the old loop's defect O3 (feasibility became a refusal) is answered by eligibility as a typed fact with ineligible entries kept on the record; the 2026-09-19 guards ruling by the register's per-row cases and the owner's "decide when a flight reaches it"; typed absence by the `:not-built` boxes and the nonfinite-G work; no catalog by the outer cascade choosing from the field rather than from a hand list. The theory has not shifted; the self-similarity (the outer cascade choosing (target, step) as the inner chooses (pattern, attempt)) is the same theory at two grains and is carried as a tension.

**Rationale citation.** No numbered war-room ruling (`war-room/wr-N`) was found to apply; the rulings this design rests on are Joe's, recorded verbatim in PROOF-2a's draft: guards presumed unwanted (2026-09-19); no catalog, the outer loop is the machine's (c2061e27); no field or refusal without a case, every written mission feasible, holes closed singly before wiring (2026-09-25 ~04:15Z); a requisition is a different semantic layer and creation makes a pending object eligible (~17:40Z, ~18:05Z).

**Trade-offs.** Conformance is textual and heuristic, so the map can be wrong in ways the checker cannot see (a field named in a comment; a read/write it cannot classify); we accepted detect-level over by-construction because the verifier for the former exists and the latter is a priced hole. Set equality on expected findings makes every wiring commit touch the map, a cost paid for never losing a finding silently. The heuristic is off by default so the control stays byte-identical, at the price of one explicit flag in the registered test. The outer cascade waits on a decision, so the first flight is against a hand-placed target rather than a chosen one. The checker stays a bb script called by shell-out, one checker rather than a port, at the cost of a process boundary on the flight's path.

**Generalisation.** The method (a component map with declared reads and writes, expected findings per unwired part, a registered checker run as the warrant, a trace per exemplar) applies to any machine assembled from tested parts: the APM pipeline's map already exists in the same form, and the lifecycle's own wiring-diagram rule names the checker. What would change elsewhere: the field vocabulary, the repos under `:repos`, and the spike that stands in for N-run.

**Plain-language argument.** Every part of the machine has been built and tested on its own, and none of them is connected. We drew a map that says, for each part, what it reads and what it writes, and a checker compares that map with the real code and lists exactly what is still not connected. Connecting a part means making its item disappear from that list, in the same change as the code. Three parts do not exist yet, and each is a short hop between two parts that do: the step that has a seat carry out the chosen plan and writes down what it did, the call that checks it, and the chooser that picks the next mission; the first two are built now and the chooser once its rule is decided.
