### VERIFY (shape as drafted before entry; kept for its BOM and pre-check)

Joe, 2026-09-25 ~23:05Z: "VERIFY would rely on the diagram prover doing its thing." The lifecycle says the same in its own words: the process row of the Specification Bill of Materials names `futon3c.diagramprover.wiring` as the verifier that exists (tool status 2026-08-15: five checks over declared ownership maps; "honest grade: detect-level checks over authored, conformance-kept declarations; by-construction unconstructibility remains the Lean/engine layer's territory"). For this mission the registered namespace `futon3c.diagramprover.wm-flight-wiring-test` over `wm-flight-wiring.edn` IS the structural verification, and a row is verified when its expected finding is gone from the map and its test box conforms.

**Bill of materials** (one row per aspect; a level may be named only where a verifier exists; no "not blocking"):
| aspect | characteristic defect | formalism and level for this mission |
|---|---|---|
| claim | confounding, non-transport | the wiring capability-proof note (TOP, nodes N0..N-run, warrant-state table; causal reading as in `capability-proof-apm`): skeleton next sitting (claude-10) |
| data / artifact | malformed or unfalsifiable record | the click and enactment records: Lean shapes in PROOF-2a's modules plus the W₀..Wₜ checkers (Clojure validators); each row's test box pins the field it writes |
| process | dangling wire, unowned field, unreachable state | the diagramprover run, registered (test-registry-b9f1a1a2 at fbb094f3): `written-never-read`, `read-never-written`, `multiply-written`, `conformance` under `{:heuristic? true}` at var grain, the trace checks, the `:not-built` absence checks, N0's load-closure check; detect-level. Priced hole: by-construction grade is not reachable here; revisit trigger: when M-diagramprover's WS-D line lands, or when a second written-but-not-wired finding survives a wiring commit |
| loop | act succeeded, measurement inadmissible | the flight organs (witness W₀..Wₜ, settled read, admissibility) over a real flight's record: N-run; verified only by the spike below |
| decision | wrong thing for unexamined reasons | the ten IF/HOWEVER/THEN/BECAUSE in DERIVE and PSR-1..4; the owner's per-row refusal decisions in the register |

**Structural verification against the lifecycle's list.** Completeness, coverage and no-orphan inputs: `written-never-read` / `read-never-written` = [] on wired rows, `multiply-written` = [] (invariants 1-2). Spec coverage: `conformance` (declaration ↔ source, both directions). Type safety, timescale ordering, exogeneity and compositional closure: NOT checked by the diagramprover; the verifiers that exist are futon5's `ct/mission.clj` validators (`validate-type-safety` :248, `validate-timescale-ordering` :311, `validate-exogeneity` :350, `validate-closure` :386, found in MAP), which take futon5's mission-diagram format, not this map's. Decision to record here, not defer unpriced: either a projection of `wm-flight-wiring.edn` into that format so those four validators run over the live artifact (a small packet; the I4 exogeneity question is the one MAP flagged as worth asking of this map: can a fast tick write a field that constrains itself?), or a priced decline with the trigger "before the first flight's record is matched to the trace". Owner's call at VERIFY entry.

**Prototype / spike.** What conformance cannot verify is the loop: that a flight produces a record with every clause's fields filled or typed-absent. The spike is the first flight of M-autoclock-in once rows 0-10 are wired (the hand-placed target, c2061e27), its record matched step by step to the `:traces` entry: N-run's witness. The refusal register (flight + tick, 4(a)) is what makes that spike's stopping points known in advance.

**Completion-criteria pre-check** (IDENTIFY): (i) a checkable click record from one flight: covered by rows 0-10 and the register; (ii) the outer cascade's selection with the draw and seed on the record: covered by row 1 as designed, PENDING Joe's decision on OUTER-CASCADE-D; (iii) every row with its own test and warrant, no hole closed by wiring: covered by the test boxes, the matrix and the set-equality discipline; (iv) efficiency per flight: 4(a) enumerated (flight + tick; post-choice on first reach), 4(b) the click record's required fields grow only by a row stating its case (checkable as the set of fields the map's boxes write), 4(c) rulings per flight counted at the flight, 4(d) wall time per tick against the outer cascade's ~20 s construction baseline, measured at the flight.

**Fidelity check.** Tripwires exist for all preserve entries in place (12) and are named for the rest (13 to write, each by its row's test box); adapt entries carry their compatibility assertions (row 4's chain case; row 1's no-data case).

**Decision log (verification-time revisions of DERIVE so far).** The checker's thread-first classification (58f1a8cb) removed two expected findings from the map (fbb094f3); the direction findings are behind an explicit flag; unbuilt code is stated as a `:not-built` box with its intended site asserted absent, reserving `:var-not-found` for regressions; the exemplar trace is a field on the map, not a file beside it.

Neither the gate nor the registry check is a box on the map (the gate sits after selection), so the map is unchanged. One dirty tree left beside it: `observation-checks-test`'s warrant test-registry-63529e15 is `:warrant? false` (29/144, 2 failures) because kimi-1's live-pin test `c8-live-command-lookup-observes-the-same-entry-as-the-namespace-lookup` reads the live registry, which now answers a newer entry (96df1637… by namespace, nothing by command) than its pin; untouched by this change; a pin that moves whenever the registry gains an entry is the wrong pin shape: LIVE-PIN-I DONE (claude-13, futon2 be6cc7a7, warrant test-registry-153a738b, observation-checks-test 29/147; claude-8: one path on main, the pinned id in the test): the test pins entry test-registry-204346f5 by id (claude-8's 04:06Z run of futon3c.test-registry-test, a `:run` record whose `:command` is the exact vector, so one record is both the namespace entry and the command-form entry), asserts it resolves as a `:run` with that command, that the command lookup and the namespace lookup return the same entry, that the entry carries the pinned command, and that it is no older than the pinned run (neither lookup can return the pinned id itself once a newer run exists; the claim tested is that the two lookups agree, the pin establishing that a command-form entry exists rather than assuming it); today both return 96df1637 by namespace and by command, so the earlier 'nothing by command' no longer holds; the no-command-form branch records `{:absent :no-command-form-entry}` and is not taken; a nonexistent pinned id fails with one typed assertion naming the id, no nil comparison. One pin of the same wrong shape left in that test, outside the packet: the ledger's last build marker asserted as `:scanned 3200 :registry-entries 3200 :complete? true`, which fails as soon as the ledger is rebuilt over a larger registry; CLOSED (LIVE-PIN-I2, claude-13, futon2 c6fb2b76, warrant test-registry-842d32f1, observation-checks-test 30/148; claude-8: one path on main, no '3200' left in the test, the marker-writing lines read): the marker's only identity is its `:at` timestamp (the `:namespace-ledger-built` marker has no id; stamped at futon3c test_registry.clj:1209-1213; the ledger is append-only, a rebuild adds a new marker and leaves the old one), so the test pins `:at "2026-09-25T03:23:03.538975798Z"`, the build the C8 pin was taken against, and asserts via `complete-marker-failure` that it is `:complete? true` with `:scanned` a positive integer equal to `:registry-entries`, printing a typed reason with the counts on failure; bad cases as a new test on constructed markers: incomplete, unequal counts, a timestamp not in the ledger (`{:absent :pinned-marker-not-in-ledger}`). M-autoclock-in's relation row stays with Joe or the receipt's owner.

**Pre-spike (WM-PRESPIKE-I, claude-10; futon3c d85b5941 the checker, warrant test-registry-130501e6, proof2a-wc-check-test 4/37; futon2 d30dde9b the flight's W_c call test, warrant test-registry-65b04eaf, 7/19; claude-8: record check holds, ran the checker's test once in futon3c's own JVM, green; no map change, the checker siteless).** `check-c` reads a hand record exactly as before when `[:decision :selection-certificate :candidate-derivations]` is present (click-001's four output modes byte-identical to f8fd0bc4); otherwise it reads the tick's run record at `[:decision :selection-certificate :candidates n :id]`, the cascade action's `:precedence` as pattern maps with target-qualified `:produces` and its `:interpretation-receipts` by pattern id, with `[:decision :selection-law :candidate]` (row 9) as the join and `[:decision :chosen]` beside it; a pattern with no receipt fails naming the pattern; neither key → `{:status :join-unverifiable :reason "… carries neither …"}`. Fixtures reduced from a real run record (`tick-run-record-2026-09-24-a4b4fc78…`, its sha and reduction in the header, the selection-law candidate added since the record predates f498f3f3) plus an enactment in `enact-fn`'s shape; the consistent pair gives `[]`, a `:C9` candidate the mismatch failure, no receipts the failure naming `:apparatus/one-authority-per-question`; the real checker on that pair gives `[]` reaching `increment` and counting 1 (fixtures read from futon3c by absolute path, outside the warrant). FINDING: no real enactment of today's candidates can pass W_c, since none of their patterns declares `:grain`, so `enact-fn` records `:grain-not-declared` and the checker fails X_c(d); the consistent fixture passes only because it was given click-001's role grain (its header says so); E will count 0 from every enactment until candidates carry a grain pattern (H-interp: the interpretation vocabulary needs a grain declaration per pattern; recorded, the owner's).

**The spike's plan (claude-10's three answers, ~20:45Z).** (1) The dispatch function `agency-dispatch-step!` beside `agency-answer-fn` comes AFTER the spike, since the first flight will not reach it; its refusals in the same commit, each typed on the flight: `{:failed {:reason :seat-not-on-roster}}`, `{:failed {:reason :requisition-missing}}` (the Kimi line `agency-answer-fn` already writes), `{:failed {:reason :job-not-terminal-by-deadline :job-id …}}` (`poll-job!` never abandons a job, so the step needs its own deadline). A gap to close BEFORE the spike: `enact-fn` calls `dispatch-step!` without checking it is configured, so a decision with no dispatch function would throw rather than record `{:absent :no-dispatch-configured}`, a two-line guard. (2) `run-flight!` does not yet pass `enact-fn`, `wc-fn`, the library root or the served-by options, so ONE MORE PACKET precedes the spike, WM-DRIVER-I: flags `--checker`, `--bb`, `--library-root`, `--field-entry <edn>`; `run-flight!` builds `enact-fn` over the click's run record and `wc-fn` with the checker; the guard. Then the command: `clojure -M -m futon2.aif.flight-driver M-autoclock-in --seat <seat> --repo futon3c --path holes/missions/M-autoclock-in.md --checker /home/joe/code/futon3c/holes/labs/M-futon-seams/exemplar/proof2a_check.clj --bb bb --library-root /home/joe/code/futon3/library --max-clicks 1 --run`; the store is `wi/default-store`, so the spike writes under data/, which the spike bell authorises; the seat is named at the bell; the flight asks it real questions in the read and ask steps. The record on today's target: `:target-source :hand-placed`; read step: a criteria request (the entry is `:criteria-not-stated`), then if criteria publish, locator requests and a constraints request; served-by `{:absent :no-instances-anchor}` with proposals `{:absent :no-quotes}` and the text sha; ask step: one interpretation request per want, each published, rejected, declined, or pending with its job id; one click abstaining at assembly on `:no-admitted-interpretation` if nothing published, or `:universe-not-admitted` when a token lacks a locator (the register's walk); `:enactments [{:enactment {:absent :no-decision}}]`; `:publication-observed` not reached; status `:no-progress`, or `:not-a-target-yet` if the criteria reading raises only questions, or `:awaiting-answer` if an ask is pending at the click. (3) The relation row: the first flight CAN reach scoring in its first click, since read and ask both run before it; if the seat publishes criteria, locators and interpretations in that step, assembly passes and scoring refuses `:class-unknown-no-scalar-g` at the missing row, a tick refusal recorded as the click's abstention, the flight ending with an accurate record; so the row is not a precondition for the first flight's record but is one for any flight of M-autoclock-in to reach a decision, Joe's or the receipt owner's call; claude-10 would fly the spike without it and let the record show where it stopped; claude-8 adopts that.

**Spike defects (three, all in the wiring claude-10 built; each a small fix with its own test box and warrant, WM-SPIKE-FIX-I):** (A) `flight.clj:231` records the click's abstention as `(select-keys abstention [:kind :missing :declines])`, dropping the `:status` and `:detail` that `http-click-fn` (`flight_runner.clj:318-321`) carries from the server's 4xx body, so a refused click's reason never reaches the record; and `:detail` itself keeps only `[:error :message :rejected]`, not the preflight's `:unready` seats. (C) `issue-request` (`flight_runner.clj:138-147`) catches an ExceptionInfo and returns `{::refused (:interpretation/refusal (ex-data e))}`; when the exception carries no such key the value is nil, `(if-let [r (::refused issued)] …)` at `:211` falls through, and the map `{::refused nil}` is handed to the seat as the request, the exception's message and data lost: an absence read as a value (PROOF-2 register AR-46). Which exception the three ask-step requests hit is not on the record; the fix records the exception (message, ex-data) as the typed refusal and stops. Not a defect of the wiring: the cast default (Joe's instruction to claude-10 stands); the per-target horizon (recorded above); the four `:universe-not-admitted` rejections (the register's walk, given B).

**What the second flight found (claude-8, from the record and the source).** (1) WHY NO UNIVERSE, AS FIRST WRITTEN (corrected below by REFUSAL-REGISTER-D6): a target's fact universe is built only from a hand-written cascade-source declaration (`cascade_sources.clj:275,284`: `observe-facts (:facts d) (:locators d)` maps each declared fact token to true/false/:unknown through its locator); the machine's reading of a lifecycle mission produces wants, constraints and locators but no `:facts`, and `target-view` reads `(:universe wants)`, which no reader writes (`flight_runner.clj:231`), so both the flight's ask-step constructions and the tick's assembly refuse `:universe-not-admitted :missing :universes` for every target the machine reads. The register's row 13 says the locator request is the route out; it is not, while facts have no machine writer. This is the next wiring row, and it begins with a definition (Clause T's universe for a read mission: the extracted criterion tokens observed through their published locators, `:unknown` where none, or something else), owner claude-10 with Joe; a discovery packet, not a build. (2) TWO REGISTRIES UNREACHABLE INSIDE THE FLIGHT, both infrastructure, both real at 23:30Z: futon1b :7073 (pid 2324578) answers `/` and `/api/alpha/health` with 404 in under a millisecond and does not answer `/api/alpha/entities?type=mission&limit=1` within 10 s, so the substrate-2 mission registry that `wi/request!`'s construction reads is hung, not down (a restart is Joe's call); and `/api/alpha/test-registry/latest?namespace=…` on :7070 answers 200 in 5.66 s (`scanned 3200`) while the C8 check's client timeout is 5000 ms (`observation_checks.clj:207`), so every C8 locator check refused `:registry-unreadable :status :unreachable`, a timeout read as unreachability. (3) TWO DROPPED REASONS, the shape of defect A: `observation_checks.clj:208` keeps the exception message as `:body` and `:211` refuses without it; `issue-request`'s `:construction-threw` (9eed8b7e) records the outer message and `:data-keys` but not `(ex-cause e)`, so the substrate's own error (why the entities query failed) is on no record. Both small fixes with test boxes, WM-SPIKE-FIX-II. (4) OFF THE REGISTER, four kinds reached: `:registry-unreadable` (`observation_checks.clj:211,215,242,249,257,318`), `:check-refused` (the locator rejection reason wrapping it), `:owner-constraint-violated` (`want_interpretation.clj:214`), `:substrate-unreachable` (`mission_registry.clj:446`, arriving as `:construction-threw`); REFUSAL-REGISTER-D6 to claude-3. **REFUSAL-REGISTER-D6 DONE (claude-3, read-only; futon2 89a7b9ee, one file, explicit path; claude-8: record check holds; verified `mission_criteria.clj:134-138` and the 0-of-100 count).** Rows 21 `:registry-unreadable` (eight sites, not six: `:322`, `:326` added; case AIF-validity stated at `:200-202`; both C8 clients dropped the message, both fixed at 367be490), 22 `:owner-constraint-violated` (`want_interpretation.clj:207-214`, carrier `:269-270`; case: the owner's constraint read from the text applied to the machine's proposal), 23 `:substrate-unreachable` (`mission_registry.clj:443-448`); `:check-refused` is a wrapper, not a kind (`mission_reading.clj:176-177` carries the check's refusal whole). The off-register count from the second flight is zero. CORRECTION OF FINDING (1): `:universe-not-admitted` has two branches, `:198` (`:missing :universes`, an empty universe) and `:217-219` (`:missing :locators`, tokens without a checkable locator); row 13 cited the first and described the second. And the universe has a machine writer: `mission_criteria/wants` (`:134-138`) builds `:universe` over the criteria that carry a `**Met when:**` declaration (each a C4 locator observed through `check-decl-in-file`), `flight_runner.clj:231` passes it, and the war machine merges it into `[:sources :universes target]` (claude-3: `war_machine.clj:6050`), which `:197` reads. M-autoclock-in carries zero `Met when:` lines, and so do all 100 lifecycle missions under futon3c `holes/missions/` (claude-8, `grep -l 'Met when'`), so for every machine-read mission the universe is `{}` and the tick stops at `:198`. The hole is therefore: the universe writer is keyed on an authored `Met when:` declaration that no mission has, while the read step's published locators (C8, from the seat) feed `:locators` and not `:universe`. Whether to author the lines or widen the writer to observe criteria through published locators is the definition question; WM-UNIVERSE-D was sent on the earlier premise and is amended in-thread.

**WM-UNIVERSE-D DONE (claude-10, read-only; futon2 98ea4e40 `proof2/packets/WM-UNIVERSE-D.md`; claude-8: one file, explicit path, no code; spot-checked `flight.clj:83-87` and `http.clj:8955-8957`; verified the staleness in the run record and repaired it).** THE SECOND FLIGHT'S CLICK RAN A STALE TICK: the tick run record's `:runner/source` (`full_loop_runner.clj:492-516`, `runner-source-drift`, identity `:source-digest-at-namespace-load`) marks eight namespaces `:stale` in the serving JVM at click time: `futon2.report.war-machine` (loaded 2026-09-24 04:52Z, sixteen hours before futon2 95aa28b2 added `flight-assembly-input`), `policy`, `cascade-problems`, `cascade-sources`, `efe`, `interpretation-construction`, `cascade-habit-store`, `scoring-input-receipts`. So the tick ignored the flight-edn entirely and abstained over 282 targets on its own sources; none of rows 1-15's tick-side wiring was exercised by that click, and claude-8's sentence above ("the click ran the tick's lane … two boxes further") holds only for the flight side. The fidelity tripwire did its job: the record says what code ran. The flight side ran HEAD: its ask-step constructions passed the empty-universe check and refused at `:218` (`:missing :locators`, 7 of 9 wanted tokens without a checkable locator), not `:198`. **The six answers:** (1) `observe-facts` maps an unlocated token to `:unknown`; assembly accepts `:unknown` values, refuses an empty universe at `:198`, and refuses at `:218` any token the problem reads with no checkable locator (P5, `:213-217`); the constructor reads `:unknown` as not established. (2) The store holds 11 extracted criteria, 2 published C8 locators (`h1dae…`, `h203c…`), 6 constraint edges; the tokens are the same kind as a declaration's `:facts` (both exit conditions), differing only in observation class and count located (2 of 9). (3) HEAD already writes the located part of the candidate universe (`flight.clj:85`); walked on this record with a current tick, the click refuses next at `:201` `:no-admitted-interpretation`, every trial interpretation stopping at `:218` on the 7 unlocated tokens; adding them as `:unknown` changes nothing, since `:218` checks locators, not values. (4) The write is flight-side (`flight.clj:70,85` → `flight_runner.clj:231`) and the flight-edn carries it into the tick (`http.clj:8956` → `full_loop_runner.clj:4590` → `war_machine.clj:7238` → the merge at `:6050`); a tick-side write is needed only outside a flight, the outer cascade. (5) The definition question for Clause T: may tokens no check can observe enter the universe as `:unknown`, reversing `:218` for read missions? Yes admits a read mission with no new contract but builds cascades toward wants whose production can never be observed, and the progress measure reads "no progress" where the truth is "unobservable"; no keeps the contract already written, one checkable locator per wanted token, and the map unchanged. Register: `:class-unknown-no-scalar-g` needs a T row in §D2.2 (claude-3, D8). **THE DEFINITION QUESTION FOR JOE (Clause T): a machine-read target has no facet-corpus relation row by construction, so what gives a flight target its relation?** The options as the code stands: (a) a relation row authored per target in `commit-facets-v1.json` (the receipt owner's; nine exist); (b) a machine reading of the relation from the mission text at click time, with a cued receipt, on the same footing as criteria and constraints (the compute-on-the-fly rule); (c) a class other than `:unknown` for a target with no row, which is a value standing in for an absence and is refused by the typed-absence rule. Horizon and beta are a lesser question: scoring a flight target in the family's declared values or its own computed ones costs nothing new either way; only the relation decides whether any flight click can reach selection. The fifth flight waits on it. **JOE'S RULING (2026-09-26 ~01:50Z, verbatim): "'I do not know how this relates to anything' is not a good answer in a world in which XTDB exists and we furthermore have an embedding of patterns and missions. So, if *you want to know* what M-autoclock-in is related to you can readily find out."** claude-8 found out (01:55Z-02:05Z), from what the machine already holds: (1) the mission text's `## Relations` section names C-substrate-completion §8.1 (this mission is the automation layer over its manual clock-in), the REPL clock-in hydra (the override surface), the mention map, E-the-dark-tower-2 (turns as typed processes), and M-operational-vocabulary (futon2, "a downstream consumer of this turn→mission link"); (2) the substrate (futon1b): the entity `38569df0-b95f-410f-bfbd-b227dbe0aaa8` (`mission|M-autoclock-in`) carries `:mission/relation "relates-to"` and its open holes; no `relations` rows from or to it (14 s per query); one hyperedge, `mission-scope/map-item` for a points-de-fuite map finding, with it as a mission end; (3) the structure embedding pinned under the facets file's `:embedding` key (`futon6/data/mission-structure-embed/`, 198 missions × 161 dims of pattern vocabulary and phases): nearest neighbours substrate-metric 0.736, war-machine-vsatarcs-interop 0.683, war-machine-first-outing 0.549, webarxana 0.507, intent-curvature 0.496, the-perfect-crime 0.490; of the nine classified targets the nearest is aif4iad 0.378 (associated, WM); to the war-machine mission itself 0.007 (the embedding is structural, not topical). `classify-target` (`focus_receipt.clj:98-139`) derives a T- target's relation through its ticket's parent with `:derived-via`, and an M- target only from a direct row, else `:unknown` ("never guessed", codex-20); the embedding is recorded as `:authority :presence-only` (`:141-152`). ## DOCUMENT (note, ahead of its phase)

The mission's working page uses the M-futon-seams presentation (Joe, 2026-09-25: zone/wip/seams.html is the style for a wiring-focused mission): the mission text as the main column, margin notes anchored to exact spans at a recorded sha, a phase table read from each exit criterion against evidence, figures in their own lane. The generator `scripts/seams_mission_page.py` takes `--mission` and `--lab` since futon3c dc86c962 (claude-1; the M-futon-seams page byte-identical before and after, sha256 7be3b52d…; a non-mission refused with nothing written). `holes/labs/M-wm-wiring/` holds only a README: `annotations.edn` and `lifecycle.edn` are the owner's to write as the rows are worked, and until then the page states both absences (empty margin; every phase "not read" with its criterion quoted, "exit met" nowhere). Suggested annotation columns for this mission: (a) the component the row calls, with sha and warrant; (b) the PROOF-2a clause the row exercises. The margin is sized for two columns; if the mission stays annotation-light the measure is worth revisiting (claude-1).
